# Proposed OEIS comments: A250431 to A251255

One section per OEIS entry: the date the result was obtained, the paper that proves it, what the entry still records as unsettled, and the text proposed for posting on that entry.

**Nothing here has been posted.** See [../SUBMITTING.md](../SUBMITTING.md).

---

## A250654

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A250654>  
Result obtained: **6 September 2026**  
Paper: [3123](../papers/3001-3500/3123-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 3*a(n-1) - 2*a(n-2); also a(n) = 2^(n-1)*36 + (5*2^(n-1)-1)*6 + 2^(n+1).
> Empirical g.f.: 2*x*(32 - 29*x) / ((1 - x)*(1 - 2*x)). - _Colin Barker_, Nov 15 2018

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The conjectured generating function is correct. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix on 128 states, and the generating function of a walk count is 1/det(I - xM) times a polynomial, so it is a ratio of two polynomials whose degrees are at most the number of states. The conjectured expression is another such ratio, of known degree, and two of them are equal as soon as enough coefficients agree: cross-multiplying leaves a polynomial whose degree is bounded, so it vanishes once that many coefficients match. Enough coefficients were compared in exact arithmetic. It follows that a(n) satisfies the linear recurrence whose coefficients are read off the denominator, which this entry does not currently record. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A250737

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A250737>  
Result obtained: **6 September 2026**  
Paper: [3155](../papers/3001-3500/3155-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 3*a(n-1) - 2*a(n-2); a(n) = 2^(n+1) + 14.
> Empirical g.f.: 2*x*(9 - 16*x) / ((1 - x)*(1 - 2*x)). - _Colin Barker_, Nov 16 2018

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The conjectured generating function is correct. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix on 16 states, and the generating function of a walk count is 1/det(I - xM) times a polynomial, so it is a ratio of two polynomials whose degrees are at most the number of states. The conjectured expression is another such ratio, of known degree, and two of them are equal as soon as enough coefficients agree: cross-multiplying leaves a polynomial whose degree is bounded, so it vanishes once that many coefficients match. Enough coefficients were compared in exact arithmetic. It follows that a(n) satisfies the linear recurrence whose coefficients are read off the denominator, which this entry does not currently record. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A250806

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A250806>  
Result obtained: **6 September 2026**  
Paper: [3031](../papers/3001-3500/3031-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 6*a(n-1) - 11*a(n-2) + 6*a(n-3); a(n) = (126*3^n - 99*2^n + 20)/2.
> Empirical g.f.: x*(100 - 221*x + 141*x^2) / ((1 - x)*(1 - 2*x)*(1 - 3*x)). - _Colin Barker_, Nov 20 2018

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The conjectured generating function is correct. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix on 27 states, and the generating function of a walk count is 1/det(I - xM) times a polynomial, so it is a ratio of two polynomials whose degrees are at most the number of states. The conjectured expression is another such ratio, of known degree, and two of them are equal as soon as enough coefficients agree: cross-multiplying leaves a polynomial whose degree is bounded, so it vanishes once that many coefficients match. Enough coefficients were compared in exact arithmetic. It follows that a(n) satisfies the linear recurrence whose coefficients are read off the denominator, which this entry does not currently record. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A250837

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A250837>  
Result obtained: **2 September 2026**  
Paper: [191](../papers/0001-0500/0191-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical recurrence of order 70 (see link above)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence in the linked file is correct, and it is the one below. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 243 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that, and Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one. Its order is 70, the order stated here, and so is the order of every tail. Any recurrence of that order the sequence satisfies therefore has the same characteristic polynomial, so it is this one. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

The conjectured expression is another such ratio, of known degree, and two of them are equal as soon as enough coefficients agree: cross-multiplying leaves a polynomial whose degree is bounded, so it vanishes once that many coefficients match. Enough coefficients were compared in exact arithmetic. It follows that a(n) satisfies the linear recurrence whose coefficients are read off the denominator, which this entry does not currently record. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026

## A250923

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A250923>  
Result obtained: **2 September 2026**  
Paper: [164](../papers/0001-0500/0164-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical recurrence of order 81 (see link above)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence in the linked file is correct, and it is the one below. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 243 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that, and Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one. Its order is 81, the order stated here, and so is the order of every tail. Any recurrence of that order the sequence satisfies therefore has the same characteristic polynomial, so it is this one. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A251006

Result obtained: **2 September 2026**  
Paper: [152](../papers/0001-0500/0152-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical recurrence of order 87 (see link above)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence in the linked file is correct, and it is the one below. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 243 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that, and Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one. Its order is 87, the order stated here, and so is the order of every tail. Any recurrence of that order the sequence satisfies therefore has the same characteristic polynomial, so it is this one. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A251207

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A251207>  
Result obtained: **2 September 2026**  
Paper: [198](../papers/0001-0500/0198-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical recurrence of order 65 (see link above)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence in the linked file is correct, and it is the one below. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 729 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that, and Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one. Its order is 65, the order stated here, and so is the order of every tail. Any recurrence of that order the sequence satisfies therefore has the same characteristic polynomial, so it is this one. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A251217

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A251217>  
Result obtained: **2 September 2026**  
Paper: [208](../papers/0001-0500/0208-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical recurrence of order 62 (see link above)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence in the linked file is correct, and it is the one below. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 128 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that, and Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one. Its order is 62, the order stated here, and so is the order of every tail. Any recurrence of that order the sequence satisfies therefore has the same characteristic polynomial, so it is this one. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```
